NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS’ MINUTES 
TUESDAY, February 11, 2014 
 
Present: Sylvia Allen, Supervisor; Dawnafe Whitesinger, Supervisor; David Tenney, Supervisor; Jesse 
Thompson, Chairman. Jonathan M. Nez, Supervisor was absent. 
9:01 a.m.  Call To Order: Pledge of Allegiance and Invocation led by Supervisor Tenney. 
CALL TO THE PUBLIC : Terry Hill from VFW  Post 9907 in Show Low addressed the Board regarding the 
2014 VFW National Veterans Priority Goals and the Arizona State  Legislature 2014 Military Veteran related 
proposed legislation. Mr. Hill discussed  SB1312 at the State Legislature,  which relates to military aff airs. Mr. 
Hill indicated he will go before the Legislature to speak to the Committee on Commerce, Energy and Military 
tomorrow and also before the Rules and Public Safety, Military and Regulatory Affairs Committee which relates 
to 5 veterans issues.  Mr. Hill indicated the County Attorney, Brad Carlyon and Judge Ruechel have been working 
on establishing a veteran’s court in Navajo County.  Mr. Hill discussed the issue of the burial of indigent veterans, 
those veterans that have been held by mortuaries over a year or more that have not been buried properly to receive 
military honors. 
       
            CONSENT AGENDA: a) Minutes: January 28, 2014 b) Warrant & Voucher Report over $1000 January 2014 
c) Justice Court Reports: Holbrook Precinct #1, January; Winsl ow Precinct #2, January; Snowflake Precinct #3, 
January; Kayenta Precinct #4, January; Show Low Precinct #5, January; and Pinetop-Lakeside #6, January 2014 
d) Clerk of the Superior Court Report January 2014 e) Juvenile Probation Report January 2014 f) Contract signed 
by the County Manager per Board of Supervisors Resolution: Sentinel Technologies, Inc. for County 
communications g) Reappointment of Evelyn Meadows as a regular member to the Planning & Zoning 
Commission for District I h) Reappointment of Bob H all as an alternate member to the Planning & Zoning 
Commission for District II i) Reappointment of Fred Shupla as a regular member to the Planning & Zoning 
Commission for District II j) Reappointment of Jason Hatch as an alternate member to the Planning & Zoning 
Commission for District III k) Approval of 12 Back Tax Land parcels sold over the counter in the amount of 
$5,635.57: 105-39-241; 105-39-242; 403-09-217; 105 -39-044; 105-39-045; 105 -39-046; 403-08-020; 403 -08-
021; 403-08-022; 403-08-023; 105-09-003E; and 105-56-445 l) Letter to Congresswoman Kirkpatrick thanking 
her for her support of the Homeowner Flood Insurance Affordability Act H.R. 3370 m) Sympathy Letter to Mayor 
Jeff Hill n) Thirteenth Amendment to the Revolving Credit Agreement and Letter of Declaration: Supervisor 
Tenney made a motion to approve the consent agenda as presented; motion seconded by Supervisor Allen. Vote 
unanimous approving the motion. 
BOARD OF SUPERVISORS:  
a. Presentation to the 2013 -2014 Holbrook High School Spirit Lin e: Supervisor Allen presented a plaque 
from the Navajo County Board of  Supervisors to the Holbrook High School Co-Ed Spirit Line for winning 
the 2013-2014 Spirit Line Division II AIA State Championship. They competed with the top ten schools 
in their divisio n held in Prescott Valley on January 25 th. Coaches Nichole Lozano, Carey Kester, and 
Cheryl Carlson were present. 
b. Presentation regarding Petrified Forest National Park expansion : Park Superintendent Brad Traver made 
the presentation and answe red questions of the Board. Mr. Traver discussed the Congress A ct of 2004  
which allowed the Petrified Forest to expand  and he spoke about  the progress of the expansions .  He 
indicated there have been specific additions to the park lands and stated they hope to open these additions

<!-- page 2 -->
Board of Supervisor Minutes 02-11-2014 
    Page 2 of 4 
 
by this spring.  Mr. Traver indicated they are working with the State to get lands opened for conservation 
and are working to protect the concerns of those who live there. He explained that park officials have been 
working with Mr. Homero Vela, Navajo County Public Works Director to acquire an easement from a 
private landowner to allow access to the land.  Mr. Traver discussed the science programs which they have 
incorporated. He stated that an ancient river once flowed there, a 200 million year old pond and formations 
that hold fossilized clam shells which were part of what was once a riverbed.  Mr. Traver indicated that 
there are 43,000  acres of designated wilderness and he reported last year researchers uncovered a 
phytosaur skull and found 7 stone axe heads. He stated last year they were close to approximately 700,000 
visitors, except for the federal government shutdown last fall which negatively affected the total number 
of visitors to the park . Chairman Thompson spoke about getting more people access to the park  who 
may not have the means to enjoy it. 
HUMAN RESOURCES : Consideration and possibl e approval of Personnel Actions : Kimberly Eavenson  
presented the 21 item personnel action report.  Supervisor Tenney made a motion  to approve the personnel 
actions as presented; motion seconded by Supervisor Whitesinger.  Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
NAVAJO COUNTY PUBLIC HEALTH SERVICES DISTRICT Board of Directors Session : Supervisor 
Tenney made a motion to enter into the Navajo County Public Health Services District  Board of Directors 
Session; motion seconded by Supervisor Whitesinger. Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
a. CONSENT AGENDA:   
i) Amendment to the Arizona Department of Health Services Contract Number ADHS 13 -036900 to 
provide HIV Prevention Services to the citizens of Navajo County: Supervisor Allen asked about the 
HIV program in the county. Dr. Wade Kartchner indicated that the HIV prevention services program 
in the county has not been very big in the past but he indicated this money will help fund the nursing 
personnel. Supervisor Tenney made a motion  to approve the consent agenda as presented; motion 
seconded by Supervisor Whitesinger. Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
b. Consideration and possible approval of Arizona Department of Health Services Contract Number ADHS 
14-063032, Public Health Accreditation: Dr. Wade Kartchner presented the item.  He indicated the grant 
will cover nearly $28,000 in fees necessary for accreditation as well as training and preparation costs. Dr. 
Kartchner stated they are working  towards completing the process and receiving  accreditation by 
September 2015. Supervisor Whitesinger made a motion to approve the Arizona Department of Health 
Services Contract Number ADHS 14 -063032, Public Health Accreditation; motion seconded by 
Supervisor Allen. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  Supervisor Tenney made a motion to return 
to regular session: motion seconded by Supervisor Whitesinger. Vote unanimous approving the motion.   
WIA: Consideration and possible approval of Navajo & Apache County Workforce 5 year Plan: Don Berry spoke 
as the Chairman of the Workforce I nvestment Act.  He indicate d he has served for 3 years and  stated this plan 
will bring us in c ompliance with federal g uidelines. Mr. Berry expressed thanks to the  WIA staff and James 
Menlove for their work on this program. Susan Tegmeyer, WIA Executive Director addressed the Board regarding 
the changes to the WIA program. She introduced Jarom Lewis, a new case manager and a part of her team. Ms. 
Tegmeyer stated they have requested a complete audit at the end of the month to see how WIA is  doing and for 
training purposes. Supervisor Allen and Chairman Thompson expressed thanks for the time and efforts put into 
the program and the importance to help those in need to improve their circumstances. Supervisor Tenney thanked 
Susan Tegmeyer and Don Berry for their work and welcomed Mr. Lewis to the program. Supervisor Allen made 
a motion to approve the Navajo & Apache County Workforce  5 year Plan; m otion seconded by Supervisor 
Tenney. Vote unanimous approving the motion.

<!-- page 3 -->
Board of Supervisor Minutes 02-11-2014 
    Page 3 of 4 
 
PUBLIC WORKS : Consideration and possible approval of Task Agreement Number 8 to Memorandum of 
Agreement AGN00090001 between Bureau of Indian Affairs (BIA), Navajo Region, Branch of Roads and Navajo 
County: Bill Bess presented the item and answered questions of the Board. Supervisor Allen made a motion to 
approve Task Agreement Number 8 to Memorandum of Agreement AGN00090001 between Bureau of Indian 
Affairs (BIA), Navajo  Region, Branch of Roads and Navajo County; motion seconded by Supervisor 
Whitesinger. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
COUNTY BUSINESS UPDATE : Report from Board members, County Attorney and County Manager : 
Supervisor Allen indicated she attended her 2nd Drug Court Graduation and expressed what a great program it 
is. Supervisor Allen expressed appreciation to Judge Hatch and those  in the county who spend their time and 
money to help encourage those in the drug program to recapture and regain their lives. She spoke on the fight for 
drug enforcement on the border and appreciates all that we do on a local level. Supervisor Allen indicated she 
attended the Little Colorado River RC&D Watershed Conference and stated  she enjoyed the many meetings on 
different topics. She reported she learned the importance of  keeping our watersheds and forests healthy.  
Supervisor Whitesinger stated she attended the First Things First White Mountain Apache Tribe meeting which 
addressed the importance of language preservation.  She indicated she went to the White Mountain Youth Corp 
Board meeting where they are looking for youth between the ages of 18 to 25 , who would like to participate in 
the program.  She reported t here will be a n upcoming project in Holbrook, which is  a trail around the Navajo 
County facility which will open in June.  Supervisor Whitesinger stated she attended a Gang Awareness program 
in Cibecue which helped her to see and understand what exists within a gang. She indicated she was a guest 
presenter at the  Arizona American Indian Education Youth Conference at ASU.  She  reported she presented  
statistics on Indian country regarding crime and the importance of the youth working within their communities. 
Supervisor Whitesinger indicated on Valentine’s Day she will visit Kindergarten classes to read to the children. 
Supervisor Tenney stated he attended the ribbon cutting at the Chevelon Creek Bridge and expressed thanks to 
Homero Vela and Bill Bess.  He indicated he visited two libraries with Geneva Durkee, Library District Director. 
Supervisor Tenney reported on l egislative meetings he attended regarding restoring the HURF funding and 
discussions of the  budget with CSA.  He  stated he  testified at the State Senate to encourage C ongress to 
permanently fund PILT.  He reported 4FRI is still waiting on the environmental impact statement.  Supervisor 
Tenney indicated the Bridge the Gap process and the White Mountain Stewardship Contract is still helping the 
business to go forward.  He reported he  has a hearing at the Stat e Senate tomorrow regarding this  contract.  
Chairman Thompson spoke on supporting a resolution to increase funding for Native A merican transportation 
issues and he stated Senator Carlyle Begay is  also working on this legislation .  He spoke of the  cultural 
significance associated with the  Chevelon Creek Bridge. He reported on the NACOG Economic Development 
Committee which he serves on.  Chairman Thompson indicated he is working with APS and the Little Colorado 
River Development Corporation to facilitate in planning together the best decisions to develop designated lands 
near Winslow. He stated he is looking to meet with the various Native American communities on the concern of 
the lack of not having fire departments.  He stated he will work with HUD to get this process started. 
ADJOURN At 10:01 a.m. Supervisor Tenney made a motion  to adjourn; motion seconded by Supervisor 
Allen. Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
 
 
APPROVED:  
 
Jesse Thompson, Chairman 
 
 
 
 
 
 
DATE:  February 25, 2014

Melissa W. Buckley, Clerk of the Board
